To solve the problem of disconnecting the two black nodes (H and R), we need to identify the minimum set of nodes whose removal will break all paths between H and R. Let's analyze the graph step-by-step:

1. **Identify the immediate neighbors of the black nodes H and R:**
   - H is connected to: A, B, C, I, J
   - R is connected to: B, J, Q

2. **Identify the paths between H and R:**
   - Path 1: H -> B -> R
   - Path 2: H -> J -> R
   - Path 3: H -> B -> A -> N -> O -> G -> I -> Q -> R
   - Path 4: H -> B -> E -> S -> F -> T -> Q -> R
   - Path 5: H -> I -> Q -> R

3. **Find the bottlenecks (nodes that, if removed, break multiple paths):**
   - Removing B breaks Path 1 and potentially other paths going through B.
   - Removing J breaks Path 2.
   - Removing Q breaks Paths 3 and 5.

   However, we need to ensure that removing these nodes completely disconnects H and R.

4. **Verify the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing B and J breaks Paths 1 and 2.
   - Removing Q breaks Paths 3 and 5.

   Therefore, removing nodes B, J, and Q will completely disconnect H and R.

Nodes to remove: 3, Cut Set: [B, J, Q]

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed so that the two black nodes (N and G) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between them. Let's go through the steps to find this minimum cut set:

1. **Identify the two black nodes**: N and G.
2. **Find all immediate neighbors of N and G**:
   - N is connected to B, C, and O.
   - G is connected to B, H, I, and T.

3. **Identify all paths between N and G**:
   - Path 1: N -> B -> G
   - Path 2: N -> B -> F -> T -> G
   - Path 3: N -> B -> H -> I -> G
   - Path 4: N -> B -> E -> S -> F -> T -> G
   - Path 5: N -> B -> E -> F -> T -> G
   - Path 6: N -> C -> H -> I -> G

4. **Find the minimum cut set**:
   - To disconnect N and G, we need to remove at least one node from each path.
   - By inspection, we see that removing H and B would disconnect all paths.

However, we need to find the minimal set:
   - Removing B directly disconnects Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 because it is a common node in all these paths.
   - Removing H disconnects Paths 3 and 6.
   - Alternatively, removing I also disconnects Path 3 and 6.
   - Removing T disconnects Paths 2 and 5.

By further analysis, we observe that removing B and T work together to disconnect all paths:
   - Removing B disconnects Paths 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5.
   - Removing T ensures that Paths 2 and 5 are still disconnected.
   
Thus, the minimum number of nodes to remove is 2, specifically B and T.

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, T]

   - N is connected to G and I.

   - O is connected to R.

   - S is connected to R, C, B, and E.
   - G is connected to C, T, and I.
   - C is connected to S, F, and G.
   - T is connected to G, B, and S.

1. H -> B -> E -> F -> I -> Q
2. H -> B -> E -> F -> C -> G -> I -> Q

4. H -> B -> S -> R -> Q

10. H -> N -> G -> C -> F -> I -> Q

- Remove edges (H, B) and (H, J) to cut off H from the rest of the graph.
- Remove edge (N, G) to cut off N from G.

### Verify the Solution:
- Removing (H, B) and (H, J) ensures H has no path to Q.
- Removing (N, G) ensures N (which connects to I) has no path to Q.

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 3.

     - R → J → I → Q → P

   - Another path of interest: E → S → A → N → C → G → B → T → H → I → Q → P
     - This path would be much longer.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:** The black nodes are S and R.
2. **Find all paths between S and R:** We need to trace all connecting paths between nodes S and R.
3. **Determine the minimum cut set:** We need to find the smallest set of edges whose removal will disconnect S and R.

### Paths between S and R:
- **Direct Paths:**
  - S -> E -> B -> P -> Q -> I -> J -> R
  - S -> E -> B -> P -> Q -> I -> C -> T -> G -> R
  - S -> E -> B -> P -> Q -> I -> C -> H -> R

- **Indirect Paths:**
  - S -> E -> B -> P -> Q -> I -> C -> T -> G -> O -> N -> E -> F -> C -> I -> J -> R
  - S -> E -> B -> P -> Q -> I -> C -> T -> G -> O -> N -> S
  - S -> N -> O -> G -> T -> C -> I -> J -> R
  - S -> N -> O -> G -> T -> C -> H -> R

### Remove the Minimum Edges:
To disconnect S and R completely, we need to remove edges from paths so that there are no remaining connections.

We identify critical edges:
1. **Edge (I, J):** This edge is part of multiple paths.
2. **Edge (C, I):** This edge also connects multiple paths.

1. Starting from node E:
   - E is connected to F, B, and S.

2. From F:
   - F is connected to C, T, and G.

3. From B:
   - B is connected to C, T, S, A, and N.

4. From S:
   - S is connected to A and Q.

5. From N (which connects to B):
   - N is connected to H.

Given these connections, we can determine that one possible path is:
   - E -> B -> N -> H

This path has a length of 3. Let's ensure it is the shortest by checking all other possible paths:
- E -> F -> C -> T -> B -> N -> H (length of 6)
- E -> F -> C -> T -> N -> H (length of 5)
- E -> S -> A -> P -> Q -> J -> I -> H (length of 7)

The shortest path indeed is:
- E -> B -> N -> H

Thus, the answer is:
Length: 3, Path: [E, B, N, H]

   - Node \( O \) is directly connected to nodes \( C \), \( T \), and \( G \).

2. **Identify Indirect Connections:**

     - \( B \) is connected to \( C \), \( E \), \( F \), \( T \), and \( N \).

     - \( E \) is connected to \( F \) and \( R \).

     - \( C \) is connected to \( B \) and \( T \).
   - From \( O \) to \( T \):
     - \( T \) is connected to \( B \) and \( N \).
   - From \( O \) to \( G \):
     - \( G \) is connected to \( C \) and \( N \).

3. **Analyze Paths:**
   - Path through \( B \): \( S \)-\( B \)-\( C \)-\( O \)
   - Path through \( B \) and \( N \): \( S \)-\( B \)-\( N \)-\( O \)
   - Path through \( B \) and \( T \): \( S \)-\( B \)-\( T \)-\( O \)
   - Path through \( B \) and \( G \): \( S \)-\( B \)-\( N \)-\( G \)-\( O \)
   - Path through \( E \) and \( F \): \( S \)-\( E \)-\( F \)-\( B \)-\( C \)-\( O \)
   - Path through \( E \) and \( R \): \( S \)-\( E \)-\( R \)-\( F \)-\( B \)-\( C \)-\( O \)

4. **Determine Minimum Cut:**
   - To disconnect \( S \) and \( O \), we need to remove nodes that are common in all paths.
   - Removing \( B \) disconnects the paths through \( B \), \( B \)-\( C \), and \( B \)-\( N \).
   - Removing \( N \) disconnects the paths through \( B \)-\( N \) and \( G \)-\( N \).
   - Removing \( C \) disconnects the paths through \( B \)-\( C \).

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed from the graph so that the two black nodes (N and Q) are completely disconnected, we need to analyze the paths between these nodes.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - N and Q are the black nodes.

2. **Find the immediate neighbors of N and Q:**
   - N is connected to: C, G, and J.
   - Q is connected to: I and P.

3. **Trace the paths between N and Q:**
   - Path 1: N -> G -> J -> H -> I -> Q
   - Path 2: N -> G -> J -> H -> I -> P -> Q
   - Path 3: N -> C -> R -> O -> J -> H -> I -> Q
   - Path 4: N -> C -> R -> O -> J -> H -> I -> P -> Q

4. **Identify critical nodes:**
   - To disconnect N and Q, we need to remove nodes that are common in all paths.
   - The critical nodes in all paths are: G, J, H, and I.

5. **Determine the minimum cut set:**
   - Removing G, J, H, and I will disconnect N and Q.
   - However, we need the minimum number of nodes. Let's check if fewer nodes can achieve the disconnection.

6. **Simplify the cut set:**
   - Removing J alone will disconnect the paths involving G -> J -> H -> I -> Q.
   - Removing I alone will disconnect the paths involving J -> H -> I -> Q.
   - Removing H alone will disconnect the paths involving J -> H -> I -> Q.
   - Removing either I or H will break the connection between N and Q through J. However, removing both ensures all paths are broken.

Therefore, the minimum number of nodes that must be removed is 2, specifically nodes H and I.

### Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [H, I]

     - \( (S, F) \)
     - \( (F, B) \)
     - \( (B, C) \)
